        - generic
          - heading "Test runner #" [level=2, ref=e74]
            - StaticText "Test runner"
            - link "#" [ref=e350]
          - StaticText "Source Code: "
          - link "lib/test.js" [ref=e75]
          - StaticText "Added in: v18.0.0, v16.17.0"
          - group
            - DisclosureTriangle "History" [expanded=false]
              - StaticText "History"
          - paragraph
            - link "Stability: 2" [ref=e351]
            - StaticText " - Stable"
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "The "
            - code
              - StaticText "node:test"
            - StaticText " module facilitates the creation of JavaScript tests. To access it:"
          - checkbox [checked=false, ref=e76]
          - code
            - StaticText "const test = require('node:test');\n"
          - StaticText "JAVASCRIPT"
          - button "COPY" [ref=e77]
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "This module is only available under the "
            - code
              - StaticText "node:"
            - StaticText " scheme."
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "Tests created via the "
            - code
              - StaticText "test"
            - StaticText " module consist of a single function that is processed in one of three ways:"
          - list
            - listitem [level=1]
              - ListMarker "1. "
              - StaticText "A synchronous function that is considered failing if it throws an exception, and is considered passing otherwise."
            - listitem [level=1]
              - ListMarker "2. "
              - StaticText "A function that returns a "
              - code
                - StaticText "Promise"
              - StaticText " that is considered failing if the "
              - code
                - StaticText "Promise"
              - StaticText " rejects, and is considered passing if the "
              - code
                - StaticText "Promise"
              - StaticText " fulfills."
            - listitem [level=1]
              - ListMarker "3. "
              - StaticText "A function that receives a callback function. If the callback receives any truthy value as its first argument, the test is considered failing. If a falsy value is passed as the first argument to the callback, the test is considered passing. If the test function receives a callback function and also returns a "
              - code
                - StaticText "Promise"
              - StaticText ", the test will fail."
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "The following example illustrates how tests are written using the "
            - code
              - StaticText "test"
            - StaticText " module."
          - generic [ref=e78] focusable [tabindex]
            - code
              - StaticText "test('synchronous passing test', (t) => {\n  // This test passes because it does not throw an exception.\n  assert.strictEqual(1, 1);\n});\n\ntest('synchronous failing test', (t) => {\n  // This test fails because it throws an exception.\n  assert.strictEqual(1, 2);\n});\n\ntest('asynchronous passing test', async (t) => {\n  // This test passes because the Promise returned by the async\n  // function is settled and not rejected.\n  assert.strictEqual(1, 1);\n});\n\ntest('asynchronous failing test', async (t) => {\n  // This test fails because the Promise returned by the async\n  // function is rejected.\n  assert.strictEqual(1, 2);\n});\n\ntest('failing test using Promises', (t) => {\n  // Promises can be used directly as well.\n  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {\n    setImmediate(() => {\n      reject(new Error('this will cause the test to fail'));\n    });\n  });\n});\n\ntest('callback passing test', (t, done) => {\n  // done() is the callback function. When the setImmediate() runs, it invokes\n  // done() with no arguments.\n  setImmediate(done);\n});\n\ntest('callback failing test', (t, done) => {\n  // When the setImmediate() runs, done() is invoked with an Error object and\n  // the test fails.\n  setImmediate(() => {\n    done(new Error('callback failure'));\n  });\n});\n"
            - StaticText "JS"
            - button "COPY" [ref=e352]
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "If any tests fail, the process exit code is set to "
            - code
              - StaticText "1"
            - StaticText "."
        - generic
          - heading "Subtests #" [level=3, ref=e79]
            - StaticText "Subtests"
            - link "#" [ref=e353]
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "The test context's "
            - code
              - StaticText "test()"
            - StaticText " method allows subtests to be created. It allows you to structure your tests in a hierarchical manner, where you can create nested tests within a larger test. This method behaves identically to the top level "
            - code
              - StaticText "test()"
            - StaticText " function. The following example demonstrates the creation of a top level test with two subtests."
          - generic [ref=e80] focusable [tabindex]
            - code
              - StaticText "test('top level test', async (t) => {\n  await t.test('subtest 1', (t) => {\n    assert.strictEqual(1, 1);\n  });\n\n  await t.test('subtest 2', (t) => {\n    assert.strictEqual(2, 2);\n  });\n});\n"
            - StaticText "JS"
            - button "COPY" [ref=e354]
          - blockquote
            - paragraph
              - strong
                - StaticText "Note:"
              - StaticText " "
              - code
                - StaticText "beforeEach"
              - StaticText " and "
              - code
                - StaticText "afterEach"
              - StaticText " hooks are triggered between each subtest execution."
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "In this example, "
            - code
              - StaticText "await"
            - StaticText " is used to ensure that both subtests have completed. This is necessary because tests do not wait for their subtests to complete, unlike tests created within suites. Any subtests that are still outstanding when their parent finishes are cancelled and treated as failures. Any subtest failures cause the parent test to fail."
        - generic
          - heading "Rerunning failed tests #" [level=3, ref=e81]
            - StaticText "Rerunning failed tests"
            - link "#" [ref=e355]
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "The test runner supports persisting the state of the run to a file, allowing the test runner to rerun failed tests without having to re-run the entire test suite. Use the "
            - link "--test-rerun-failures" [ref=e356]
              - code
                - StaticText "--test-rerun-failures"
            - StaticText " command-line option to specify a file path where the state of the run is stored. if the state file does not exist, the test runner will create it. the state file is a JSON file that contains an array of run attempts. Each run attempt is an object mapping successful tests to the attempt they have passed in. The key identifying a test in this map is the test file path, with the line and column where the test is defined. in a case where a test defined in a specific location is run multiple times, for example within a function or a loop, a counter will be appended to the key, to disambiguate the test runs. note changing the order of test execution or the location of a test can lead the test runner to consider tests as passed on a previous attempt, meaning "
            - code
              - StaticText "--test-rerun-failures"
            - StaticText " should be used when tests run in a deterministic order."
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "example of a state file:"
          - generic [ref=e82] focusable [tabindex]
            - code
              - StaticText "[\n  {\n    \"test.js:10:5\": { \"passed_on_attempt\": 0, \"name\": \"test 1\" }\n  },\n  {\n    \"test.js:10:5\": { \"passed_on_attempt\": 0, \"name\": \"test 1\" },\n    \"test.js:20:5\": { \"passed_on_attempt\": 1, \"name\": \"test 2\" }\n  }\n]\n"
            - StaticText "JSON"
            - button "COPY" [ref=e357]
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "in this example, there are two run attempts, with two tests defined in "
            - code
              - StaticText "test.js"
            - StaticText ", the first test succeeded on the first attempt, and the second test succeeded on the second attempt."
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "When the "
            - code
              - StaticText "--test-rerun-failures"
            - StaticText " option is used, the test runner will only run tests that have not yet passed."
          - generic [ref=e83] focusable [tabindex]
            - code
              - StaticText "node --test-rerun-failures /path/to/state/file\n"
            - StaticText "BASH"
            - button "COPY" [ref=e358]

          - StaticText "Added in: v25.5.0, v24.14.0"
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "This flips the pass/fail reporting for a specific test or suite: a flagged test case must throw in order to pass, and a flagged test case that does not throw fails."
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "In each of the following, "
            - code
              - StaticText "doTheThing()"
            - StaticText " fails to return "

            - code
              - StaticText "// Assume Node.js is run with the --test-only command-line option.\n// The suite's 'only' option is set, so these tests are run.\ntest('this test is run', { only: true }, async (t) => {\n  // Within this test, all subtests are run by default.\n  await t.test('running subtest');\n\n  // The test context can be updated to run subtests with the 'only' option.\n  t.runOnly(true);\n  await t.test('this subtest is now skipped');\n  await t.test('this subtest is run', { only: true });\n\n  // Switch the context back to execute all tests.\n  t.runOnly(false);\n  await t.test('this subtest is now run');\n\n  // Explicitly do not run these tests.\n  await t.test('skipped subtest 3', { only: false });\n  await t.test('skipped subtest 4', { skip: true });\n});\n\n// The 'only' option is not set, so this test is skipped.\ntest('this test is not run', () => {\n  // This code is not run.\n  throw new Error('fail');\n});\n\ndescribe('a suite', () => {\n  // The 'only' option is set, so this test is run.\n  it('this test is run', { only: true }, () => {\n    // This code is run.\n  });\n\n  it('this test is not run', () => {\n    // This code is not run.\n    throw new Error('fail');\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe.only('a suite', () => {\n  // The 'only' option is set, so this test is run.\n  it('this test is run', () => {\n    // This code is run.\n  });\n\n  it('this test is run', () => {\n    // This code is run.\n  });\n});\n"

            - StaticText " section."
        - generic
          - heading "Test runner execution model #" [level=4, ref=e128]
            - StaticText "Test runner execution model"
            - link "#" [ref=e420]
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "When process-level test isolation is enabled, each matching test file is executed in a separate child process. The maximum number of child processes running at any time is controlled by the "
            - link "--test-concurrency" [ref=e421]
              - code
                - StaticText "--test-concurrency"
            - StaticText " flag. If the child process finishes with an exit code of 0, the test is considered passing. Otherwise, the test is considered to be a failure. Test files must be executable by Node.js, but are not required to use the "
            - code
              - StaticText "node:test"
            - StaticText " module internally."
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "Each test file is executed as if it was a regular script. That is, if the test file itself uses "
            - code
              - StaticText "node:test"
            - StaticText " to define tests, all of those tests will be executed within a single application thread, regardless of the value of the "
            - code
              - StaticText "concurrency"
            - StaticText " option of "
            - link "test()" [ref=e422]
              - code
                - StaticText "test()"
            - StaticText "."
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "When process-level test isolation is disabled, each matching test file is imported into the test runner process. Once all test files have been loaded, the top level tests are executed with a concurrency of one. Because the test files are all run within the same context, it is possible for tests to interact with each other in ways that are not possible when isolation is enabled. For example, if a test relies on global state, it is possible for that state to be modified by a test originating from another file."
        - generic
          - heading "Child process option inheritance #" [level=5, ref=e129]
            - StaticText "Child process option inheritance"
            - link "#" [ref=e423]
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "When running tests in process isolation mode (the default), spawned child processes inherit Node.js options from the parent process, including those specified in "
            - link "configuration files" [ref=e424]
            - StaticText ". However, certain flags are filtered out to enable proper test runner functionality:"
          - list
            - listitem [level=1]
              - ListMarker "• "
              - code
                - StaticText "--test"
              - StaticText " - Prevented to avoid recursive test execution"
            - listitem [level=1]
              - ListMarker "• "
              - code
                - StaticText "--experimental-test-coverage"
              - StaticText " - Managed by the test runner"
            - listitem [level=1]
              - ListMarker "• "
              - code
                - StaticText "--experimental-test-tag-filter"
              - StaticText " - Filter values are validated by the parent process and re-emitted to child processes"
            - listitem [level=1]
              - ListMarker "• "
              - code
                - StaticText "--watch"
              - StaticText " - Watch mode is handled at the parent level"
            - listitem [level=1]
              - ListMarker "• "
              - code
                - StaticText "--experimental-default-config-file"
              - StaticText " - Config file loading is handled by the parent"
            - listitem [level=1]
              - ListMarker "• "
              - code
                - StaticText "--test-reporter"
              - StaticText " - Reporting is managed by the parent process"
            - listitem [level=1]
              - ListMarker "• "
              - code
                - StaticText "--test-reporter-destination"
              - StaticText " - Output destinations are controlled by the parent"
            - listitem [level=1]
              - ListMarker "• "
              - code
                - StaticText "--experimental-config-file"
              - StaticText " - Config file paths are managed by the parent"
            - listitem [level=1]
              - ListMarker "• "
              - code
                - StaticText "--test-randomize"
              - StaticText " - Randomization is managed by the parent process and propagated to child processes"
            - listitem [level=1]
              - ListMarker "• "
              - code
                - StaticText "--test-random-seed"
              - StaticText " - Randomization seed is managed by the parent process and propagated to child processes"
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "All other Node.js options from command line arguments, environment variables, and configuration files are inherited by the child processes."
        - generic
          - heading "Collecting code coverage #" [level=3, ref=e130]

            - link "#" [ref=e425]
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "Stability: 1 - Experimental"
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "When Node.js is started with the "

              - code

            - StaticText " command-line flag, code coverage is collected and statistics are reported once all tests have completed. If the "
            - link "NODE_V8_COVERAGE" [ref=e427]
              - code

            - StaticText " environment variable is used to specify a code coverage directory, the generated V8 coverage files are written to that directory. Node.js core modules and files within "
            - code

              - code

            - StaticText " flag. If coverage is enabled, the coverage report is sent to any "

            - StaticText " via the "
            - code

            - StaticText " event."
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "Coverage can be disabled on a series of lines using the following comment syntax:"

              - StaticText "/* node:coverage disable */\nif (anAlwaysFalseCondition) {\n  // Code in this branch will never be executed, but the lines are ignored for\n  // coverage purposes. All lines following the 'disable' comment are ignored\n  // until a corresponding 'enable' comment is encountered.\n  console.log('this is never executed');\n}\n/* node:coverage enable */\n"

          - heading "Mocking #" [level=3, ref=e135]
            - StaticText "Mocking"
            - link "#" [ref=e435]
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "The "
            - code
              - StaticText "node:test"
            - StaticText " module supports mocking during testing via a top-level "
            - code
              - StaticText "mock"
            - StaticText " object. The following example creates a spy on a function that adds two numbers together. The spy is then used to assert that the function was called as expected."
          - checkbox [checked=false, ref=e136]
          - code
            - StaticText "const assert = require('node:assert');\nconst { mock, test } = require('node:test');\n\ntest('spies on a function', () => {\n  const sum = mock.fn((a, b) => {\n    return a + b;\n  });\n\n  assert.strictEqual(sum.mock.callCount(), 0);\n  assert.strictEqual(sum(3, 4), 7);\n  assert.strictEqual(sum.mock.callCount(), 1);\n\n  const call = sum.mock.calls[0];\n  assert.deepStrictEqual(call.arguments, [3, 4]);\n  assert.strictEqual(call.result, 7);\n  assert.strictEqual(call.error, undefined);\n\n  // Reset the globally tracked mocks.\n  mock.reset();\n});\n"
          - StaticText "JAVASCRIPT"
          - button "COPY" [ref=e137]
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "The same mocking functionality is also exposed on the "
            - link "TestContext" [ref=e436]
              - code
                - StaticText "TestContext"
            - StaticText " object of each test. The following example creates a spy on an object method using the API exposed on the "
            - code
              - StaticText "TestContext"
            - StaticText ". The benefit of mocking via the test context is that the test runner will automatically restore all mocked functionality once the test finishes."
          - generic [ref=e138] focusable [tabindex]
            - code
              - StaticText "test('spies on an object method', (t) => {\n  const number = {\n    value: 5,\n    add(a) {\n      return this.value + a;\n    },\n  };\n\n  t.mock.method(number, 'add');\n  assert.strictEqual(number.add.mock.callCount(), 0);\n  assert.strictEqual(number.add(3), 8);\n  assert.strictEqual(number.add.mock.callCount(), 1);\n\n  const call = number.add.mock.calls[0];\n\n  assert.deepStrictEqual(call.arguments, [3]);\n  assert.strictEqual(call.result, 8);\n  assert.strictEqual(call.target, undefined);\n  assert.strictEqual(call.this, number);\n});\n"
            - StaticText "JS"
            - button "COPY" [ref=e437]
        - generic
          - heading "Timers #" [level=4, ref=e139]
            - StaticText "Timers"
            - link "#" [ref=e438]
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "Mocking timers is a technique commonly used in software testing to simulate and control the behavior of timers, such as "
            - code
              - StaticText "setInterval"
            - StaticText " and "
            - code
              - StaticText "setTimeout"
            - StaticText ", without actually waiting for the specified time intervals."
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "Refer to the "
            - link "MockTimers" [ref=e439]
              - code
                - StaticText "MockTimers"
            - StaticText " class for a full list of methods and features."
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "This allows developers to write more reliable and predictable tests for time-dependent functionality."
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "The example below shows how to mock "
            - code
              - StaticText "setTimeout"
            - StaticText ". Using "
            - code
              - StaticText ".enable({ apis: ['setTimeout'] });"
            - StaticText " it will mock the "
            - code
              - StaticText "setTimeout"
            - StaticText " functions in the "
            - link "node:timers" [ref=e440]
            - StaticText " and "
            - link "node:timers/promises" [ref=e441]
            - StaticText " modules, as well as from the Node.js global context."
          - paragraph
            - strong
              - StaticText "Note:"
            - StaticText " Destructuring functions such as "
            - code
              - StaticText "import { setTimeout } from 'node:timers'"
            - StaticText " is currently not supported by this API."
          - checkbox [checked=false, ref=e140]
          - code
            - StaticText "const assert = require('node:assert');\nconst { mock, test } = require('node:test');\n\ntest('mocks setTimeout to be executed synchronously without having to actually wait for it', () => {\n  const fn = mock.fn();\n\n  // Optionally choose what to mock\n  mock.timers.enable({ apis: ['setTimeout'] });\n  setTimeout(fn, 9999);\n  assert.strictEqual(fn.mock.callCount(), 0);\n\n  // Advance in time\n  mock.timers.tick(9999);\n  assert.strictEqual(fn.mock.callCount(), 1);\n\n  // Reset the globally tracked mocks.\n  mock.timers.reset();\n\n  // If you call reset mock instance, it will also reset timers instance\n  mock.reset();\n});\n"
          - StaticText "JAVASCRIPT"
          - button "COPY" [ref=e141]
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "The same mocking functionality is also exposed in the mock property on the "
            - link "TestContext" [ref=e442]
              - code
                - StaticText "TestContext"
            - StaticText " object of each test. The benefit of mocking via the test context is that the test runner will automatically restore all mocked timers functionality once the test finishes."
          - checkbox [checked=false, ref=e142]
          - code
            - StaticText "const assert = require('node:assert');\nconst { test } = require('node:test');\n\ntest('mocks setTimeout to be executed synchronously without having to actually wait for it', (context) => {\n  const fn = context.mock.fn();\n\n  // Optionally choose what to mock\n  context.mock.timers.enable({ apis: ['setTimeout'] });\n  setTimeout(fn, 9999);\n  assert.strictEqual(fn.mock.callCount(), 0);\n\n  // Advance in time\n  context.mock.timers.tick(9999);\n  assert.strictEqual(fn.mock.callCount(), 1);\n});\n"
          - StaticText "JAVASCRIPT"
          - button "COPY" [ref=e143]
        - generic
          - heading "Dates #" [level=4, ref=e144]
            - StaticText "Dates"
            - link "#" [ref=e443]
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "The mock timers API also allows the mocking of the "
            - code
              - StaticText "Date"
            - StaticText " object. This is a useful feature for testing time-dependent functionality, or to simulate internal calendar functions such as "
            - code
              - StaticText "Date.now()"
            - StaticText "."
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "The dates implementation is also part of the "
            - link "MockTimers" [ref=e444]
              - code
                - StaticText "MockTimers"
            - StaticText " class. Refer to it for a full list of methods and features."
          - paragraph
            - strong
              - StaticText "Note:"
            - StaticText " Dates and timers are dependent when mocked together. This means that if you have both the "
            - code
              - StaticText "Date"
            - StaticText " and "
            - code
              - StaticText "setTimeout"
            - StaticText " mocked, advancing the time will also advance the mocked date as they simulate a single internal clock."
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "The example below show how to mock the "
            - code
              - StaticText "Date"
            - StaticText " object and obtain the current "
            - code
              - StaticText "Date.now()"
            - StaticText " value."
          - checkbox [checked=false, ref=e145]
          - code
            - StaticText "const assert = require('node:assert');\nconst { test } = require('node:test');\n\ntest('mocks the Date object', (context) => {\n  // Optionally choose what to mock\n  context.mock.timers.enable({ apis: ['Date'] });\n  // If not specified, the initial date will be based on 0 in the UNIX epoch\n  assert.strictEqual(Date.now(), 0);\n\n  // Advance in time will also advance the date\n  context.mock.timers.tick(9999);\n  assert.strictEqual(Date.now(), 9999);\n});\n"
          - StaticText "JAVASCRIPT"
          - button "COPY" [ref=e146]
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "If there is no initial epoch set, the initial date will be based on 0 in the Unix epoch. This is January 1st, 1970, 00:00:00 UTC. You can set an initial date by passing a "
            - code
              - StaticText "now"
            - StaticText " property to the "
            - code
              - StaticText ".enable()"
            - StaticText " method. This value will be used as the initial date for the mocked "
            - code
              - StaticText "Date"
            - StaticText " object. It can either be a positive integer, or another Date object."
          - checkbox [checked=false, ref=e147]
          - code
            - StaticText "const assert = require('node:assert');\nconst { test } = require('node:test');\n\ntest('mocks the Date object with initial time', (context) => {\n  // Optionally choose what to mock\n  context.mock.timers.enable({ apis: ['Date'], now: 100 });\n  assert.strictEqual(Date.now(), 100);\n\n  // Advance in time will also advance the date\n  context.mock.timers.tick(200);\n  assert.strictEqual(Date.now(), 300);\n});\n"
          - StaticText "JAVASCRIPT"
          - button "COPY" [ref=e148]
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "You can use the "
            - code
              - StaticText ".setTime()"
            - StaticText " method to manually move the mocked date to another time. This method only accepts a positive integer."
          - paragraph
            - strong
              - StaticText "Note:"
            - StaticText " This method will "
            - strong
              - StaticText "not"
            - StaticText " execute any mocked timers that are in the past from the new time."
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "In the below example we are setting a new time for the mocked date."
          - checkbox [checked=false, ref=e149]
          - code
            - StaticText "const assert = require('node:assert');\nconst { test } = require('node:test');\n\ntest('sets the time of a date object', (context) => {\n  // Optionally choose what to mock\n  context.mock.timers.enable({ apis: ['Date'], now: 100 });\n  assert.strictEqual(Date.now(), 100);\n\n  // Advance in time will also advance the date\n  context.mock.timers.setTime(1000);\n  context.mock.timers.tick(200);\n  assert.strictEqual(Date.now(), 1200);\n});\n"
          - StaticText "JAVASCRIPT"
          - button "COPY" [ref=e150]
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "Timers scheduled in the past will "
            - strong
              - StaticText "not"
            - StaticText " run when you call "
            - code
              - StaticText "setTime()"
            - StaticText ". To execute those timers, you can use the "
            - code
              - StaticText ".tick()"
            - StaticText " method to move forward from the new time."
          - checkbox [checked=false, ref=e151]
          - code
            - StaticText "const assert = require('node:assert');\nconst { test } = require('node:test');\n\ntest('setTime does not execute timers', (context) => {\n  // Optionally choose what to mock\n  context.mock.timers.enable({ apis: ['setTimeout', 'Date'] });\n  const fn = context.mock.fn();\n  setTimeout(fn, 1000);\n\n  context.mock.timers.setTime(800);\n  // Timer is not executed as the time is not yet reached\n  assert.strictEqual(fn.mock.callCount(), 0);\n  assert.strictEqual(Date.now(), 800);\n\n  context.mock.timers.setTime(1200);\n  // Timer is still not executed\n  assert.strictEqual(fn.mock.callCount(), 0);\n  // Advance in time to execute the timer\n  context.mock.timers.tick(0);\n  assert.strictEqual(fn.mock.callCount(), 1);\n  assert.strictEqual(Date.now(), 1200);\n});\n"
          - StaticText "JAVASCRIPT"
          - button "COPY" [ref=e152]
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "Using "
            - code
              - StaticText ".runAll()"
            - StaticText " will execute all timers that are currently in the queue. This will also advance the mocked date to the time of the last timer that was executed as if the time has passed."
          - checkbox [checked=false, ref=e153]
          - code
            - StaticText "const assert = require('node:assert');\nconst { test } = require('node:test');\n\ntest('runs timers as setTime passes ticks', (context) => {\n  // Optionally choose what to mock\n  context.mock.timers.enable({ apis: ['setTimeout', 'Date'] });\n  const fn = context.mock.fn();\n  setTimeout(fn, 1000);\n  setTimeout(fn, 2000);\n  setTimeout(fn, 3000);\n\n  context.mock.timers.runAll();\n  // All timers are executed as the time is now reached\n  assert.strictEqual(fn.mock.callCount(), 3);\n  assert.strictEqual(Date.now(), 3000);\n});\n"
          - StaticText "JAVASCRIPT"
          - button "COPY" [ref=e154]
        - generic
          - heading "Snapshot testing #" [level=3, ref=e155]
            - StaticText "Snapshot testing"
            - link "#" [ref=e445]
          - StaticText "Added in: v22.3.0"
          - group
            - DisclosureTriangle "History" [expanded=false]
              - StaticText "History"
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "Snapshot tests allow arbitrary values to be serialized into string values and compared against a set of known good values. The known good values are known as snapshots, and are stored in a snapshot file. Snapshot files are managed by the test runner, but are designed to be human readable to aid in debugging. Best practice is for snapshot files to be checked into source control along with your test files."
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "Snapshot files are generated by starting Node.js with the "
            - link "--test-update-snapshots" [ref=e446]
              - code
                - StaticText "--test-update-snapshots"
            - StaticText " command-line flag. A separate snapshot file is generated for each test file. By default, the snapshot file has the same name as the test file with a "
            - code
              - StaticText ".snapshot"
            - StaticText " file extension. This behavior can be configured using the "
            - code
              - StaticText "snapshot.setResolveSnapshotPath()"
            - StaticText " function. Each snapshot assertion corresponds to an export in the snapshot file."
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "An example snapshot test is shown below. The first time this test is executed, it will fail because the corresponding snapshot file does not exist."
          - generic [ref=e156] focusable [tabindex]
            - code
              - StaticText "// test.js\nsuite('suite of snapshot tests', () => {\n  test('snapshot test', (t) => {\n    t.assert.snapshot({ value1: 1, value2: 2 });\n    t.assert.snapshot(5);\n  });\n});\n"
            - StaticText "JS"
            - button "COPY" [ref=e447]
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "Generate the snapshot file by running the test file with "
            - code
              - StaticText "--test-update-snapshots"
            - StaticText ". The test should pass, and a file named "
            - code
              - StaticText "test.js.snapshot"
            - StaticText " is created in the same directory as the test file. The contents of the snapshot file are shown below. Each snapshot is identified by the full name of test and a counter to differentiate between snapshots in the same test."
          - generic [ref=e157] focusable [tabindex]
            - code
              - StaticText "exports[`suite of snapshot tests > snapshot test 1`] = `\n{\n  \"value1\": 1,\n  \"value2\": 2\n}\n`;\n\nexports[`suite of snapshot tests > snapshot test 2`] = `\n5\n`;\n"
            - StaticText "JS"
            - button "COPY" [ref=e448]
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "Once the snapshot file is created, run the tests again without the "
            - code
              - StaticText "--test-update-snapshots"
            - StaticText " flag. The tests should pass now."
        - generic

            - StaticText "const { Transform } = require('node:stream');\n\nconst customReporter = new Transform({\n  writableObjectMode: true,\n  transform(event, encoding, callback) {\n    switch (event.type) {\n      case 'test:dequeue':\n        callback(null, `test ${event.data.name} dequeued`);\n        break;\n      case 'test:enqueue':\n        callback(null, `test ${event.data.name} enqueued`);\n        break;\n      case 'test:watch:drained':\n        callback(null, 'test watch queue drained');\n        break;\n      case 'test:watch:restarted':\n        callback(null, 'test watch restarted due to file change');\n        break;\n      case 'test:start':\n        callback(null, `test ${event.data.name} started`);\n        break;\n      case 'test:pass':\n        callback(null, `test ${event.data.name} passed`);\n        break;\n      case 'test:fail':\n        callback(null, `test ${event.data.name} failed`);\n        break;\n      case 'test:plan':\n        callback(null, 'test plan');\n        break;\n      case 'test:diagnostic':\n      case 'test:stderr':\n      case 'test:stdout':\n        callback(null, event.data.message);\n        break;\n      case 'test:coverage': {\n        const { totalLineCount } = event.data.summary.totals;\n        callback(null, `total line count: ${totalLineCount}\\n`);\n        break;\n      }\n    }\n  },\n});\n\nmodule.exports = customReporter;\n"

This may lead to unexpected behavior if you intend the \"once\" value to only be used for a get operation."

          - StaticText "JAVASCRIPT"

          - StaticText "JAVASCRIPT"

          - code
            - StaticText "const assert = require('node:assert');\nconst { test } = require('node:test');\nconst nodeTimersPromises = require('node:timers/promises');\ntest('should tick five times testing a real use case', async (context) => {\n  context.mock.timers.enable({ apis: ['setInterval'] });\n\n  const expectedIterations = 3;\n  const interval = 1000;\n  const startedAt = Date.now();\n  async function run() {\n    const times = [];\n    for await (const time of nodeTimersPromises.setInterval(interval, startedAt)) {\n      times.push(time);\n      if (times.length === expectedIterations) break;\n    }\n    return times;\n  }\n\n  const r = run();\n  context.mock.timers.tick(interval);\n  context.mock.timers.tick(interval);\n  context.mock.timers.tick(interval);\n\n  const timeResults = await r;\n  assert.strictEqual(timeResults.length, expectedIterations);\n  for (let it = 1; it < expectedIterations; it++) {\n    assert.strictEqual(timeResults[it - 1], startedAt + (interval * it));\n  }\n});\n"
          - StaticText "JAVASCRIPT"

            - code
              - StaticText "timers.runAll()"
            - link "#" [ref=e561]
          - StaticText "Added in: v20.4.0, v18.19.0"
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "Triggers all pending mocked timers immediately. If the "
            - code
              - StaticText "Date"
            - StaticText " object is also mocked, it will also advance the "
            - code
              - StaticText "Date"
            - StaticText " object to the furthest timer's time."
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "The example below triggers all pending timers immediately, causing them to execute without any delay."
          - checkbox [checked=false, ref=e262]
          - code
            - StaticText "const assert = require('node:assert');\nconst { test } = require('node:test');\n\ntest('runAll functions following the given order', (context) => {\n  context.mock.timers.enable({ apis: ['setTimeout', 'Date'] });\n  const results = [];\n  setTimeout(() => results.push(1), 9999);\n\n  // Notice that if both timers have the same timeout,\n  // the order of execution is guaranteed\n  setTimeout(() => results.push(3), 8888);\n  setTimeout(() => results.push(2), 8888);\n\n  assert.deepStrictEqual(results, []);\n\n  context.mock.timers.runAll();\n  assert.deepStrictEqual(results, [3, 2, 1]);\n  // The Date object is also advanced to the furthest timer's time\n  assert.strictEqual(Date.now(), 9999);\n});\n"
          - StaticText "JAVASCRIPT"
          - button "COPY" [ref=e263]

            - code
              - StaticText "runAll()"
            - StaticText " function is specifically designed for triggering timers in the context of timer mocking. It does not have any effect on real-time system clocks or actual timers outside of the mocking environment."

            - StaticText "Context propagation with "
            - code
              - StaticText "bindStore()"
            - link "#" [ref=e603]
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "The tracing channel can be used to propagate context through test execution by binding an "
            - code
              - StaticText "AsyncLocalStorage"
            - StaticText " instance. This allows context to be automatically available in the test function and all async operations within the test."
          - generic [ref=e295] focusable [tabindex]
            - code
              - StaticText "import dc from 'node:diagnostics_channel';\nimport { AsyncLocalStorage } from 'node:async_hooks';\n\nconst testStorage = new AsyncLocalStorage();\nconst testChannel = dc.tracingChannel('node.test');\n\n// Bind context to test execution — the returned value becomes the store\ntestChannel.start.bindStore(testStorage, (data) => {\n  return { testName: data.name, startTime: Date.now() };\n});\n\n// Optionally handle errors and cleanup\ntestChannel.error.subscribe((data) => {\n  const store = testStorage.getStore();\n  console.log(`Test \"${data.name}\" failed after ${Date.now() - store.startTime}ms`);\n});\n\ntestChannel.end.subscribe((data) => {\n  const store = testStorage.getStore();\n  console.log(`Test \"${data.name}\" completed in ${Date.now() - store.startTime}ms`);\n});\n"
            - StaticText "MJS"
            - button "COPY" [ref=e604]
          - paragraph
            - StaticText "When using "
            - code
              - StaticText "bindStore()"
            - StaticText ", the context provided will be automatically propagated to the test function and all async operations within the test, without requiring any additional instrumentation in the test code."
